def propose_candidate(seed=42,budget_s=2400):
    N=8192
    a=np.arange(1,N+1,dtype=float)
    a/=a.sum()
    return [float(x) for x in a]

Maybe use **max-entropy flat convolution** via iterative proportional fitting (Dempster's algorithm). We want p*p ≤ C. Equivalent to find p with all sum probabilities low. Potential algorithm for full refine:
```
def refine_smooth(p0, deadline, rng, beta_schedule):
  p = start
  y = p.copy()
  t = 1.0
  step = 1e-4? Need scale.
  for each beta:
    for iter:
      b=conv(y)
      G,w = smooth(b,beta)
      obj = n*G
      grad = derivative
      # Lipschitz estimate: max eigenvalue of 2Q_k? For smooth matrix diag beta w; gradient Lipschitz ~ 2 beta max? But on simplex, can backtrack.
      trial = y - step*grad
      x = project(trial)
      if F(x) <= F(y) + step*norm^2/2 accept else reduce
      t_new=0.5(1+sqrt(1+4t^2)); y = x + (t/t_new-1)(x-prev)
```

def full_refine(p0, deadline, rng, beta=100000, max_iter=200000, use_momentum=True):
    p=normalize(p0)
    n=p.size
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p)
    y=p.copy(); prev=p.copy(); t=1.0
    step=1.0
    for iter:
       b=conv(y); G,w=smooth(b,beta)
       if not finite break
       s=corr_pw(y,w); g=2*s; grad=g - mean(g)
       # backtracking with smooth objective
       accepted=False
       for mult in ...:
          alpha=step*mult
          x=project(y - alpha*grad)
          # optional momentum? if no momentum, x is proposed
          xb=conv(x); Gx,_=smooth(xb,beta)
          if n*Gx < n*G - 1e-12: accept
       if accept:
          if exact score improve best
          if momentum:
             prev_x = x
             t_new=...
             y = x + (t/t_new -1)*(x - prev)  # but prev before update
             y=project(y)
          else y=x
       else step*=0.5
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget_s-90
    N=8192 (or 16384? choose 8192 for safe eval; maybe 12288 not power? Use power of two 8192)
    # generate initial candidates full N
    inits=[]
    if 'height_sequence_1' in globals(): add normalized
    add structured full starts: uniform, random smooth, exponentials, beta, sine phases, two-level step, maybe piecewise from continuous optimization? 
    score each fast_score, keep top 5
    # Phase 1: low-dim/global parametric search on 1024 length? 
       run a derivative-free or gradient on Fourier basis for D=16 for best shapes; add candidates. (Maybe optional if time)
    # Phase 2: coarse-to-fine block optimization for best candidate(s)
       best_p = top; best_score
       B_list = [4,8,16,32,64,128,256,512,1024,2048]
       for B: if n%B==0:
           # initial c from best_p, plus maybe a couple random structured c (for coarse only)
           for each start (up to 3 at high B):
              c = ...
              for beta in [10000,50000]? maybe two stages
                  c=run_block_minimize(c,n,B,beta,maxiter depending)
                  p=block_to_p; score
              update best
           # after best B, maybe convert back to full and quick full refine? 
    # Phase 3: full refine cycles
       remaining time split among beta list [20000,100000,500000] and random restarts
       for each beta:
           full_refine(best_p, subdeadline, beta)
       # active exact cycles with high beta and no momentum
    # Phase 4: final cleanup, maybe run a few last local improvements with active set, output.
```

Sort method: 1 sort 8192 ~0.001s + cumsum; maybe 5x faster. Let's implement exact projection with sorting:
```
def project_simplex(v,target=1.0):
  v=np.asarray(v,float)
  if not finite... 
  u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
  cv=np.cumsum(u)
  rho=np.nonzero(u*(np.arange(1,len(u)+1))>(cv[-1]-target))[0][-1]
  sigma=(cv[rho]-target)/(rho+1.0)
  return np.maximum(v-sigma,0.0)
```
This is O(n log n) but optimized C sort. For n=8192 maybe ~0.002. Use exact. Could also target 1 always.
We can cache? no.

def p_to_block_c(p,B):
  L=n//B
  block = p.reshape(B,L).mean(axis=1)
  block = np.maximum(block, 1e-300)
  logb = np.log(block)
  c = logb[:-1] - logb[-1]
  return np.clip(c,-8,8)
```

```
def block_obj(c,n,B,beta):
  p=block_to_p(n,B,c)
  b=fft_conv(p)
  G,w=smooth_max(b,beta)
  s=corr_pw(p,w)
  g=2*s
  Gsum=(g.reshape(B,n//B)).sum(axis=1)
  H= p.reshape(B,-1)[0]? actually H_b = p block value = block mean.
  Wavg = dot(wb, Gsum) # wb = normalized block weights
  grad = (wb / L)*(Gsum - Wavg)
  grad = n*grad
  # finite
  return n*G, grad[:-1]
```
Need wb. In block_to_p, we compute wb. To avoid recomputing, return p, wb? But objective function recomputes. Efficient enough. Could write internal helper `block_p_and_wb(c,n,B)` top-level to avoid duplication. But no closures, can define top-level.

def block_p_and_wb(c,n,B):
  L=n//B
  z=...
  wb=exp(z-max)/sum
  p=np.repeat(wb/L,L)
  return p, wb
```

```
p = start; y=p; prev=None; t=1
for it:
  grad at y
  alpha line search for x = project(y - alpha*grad) (no momentum in trial)
  if not accept: step*=0.5; continue
  prev_x = x
  t_new = 0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t*t))
  y = x + (t/t_new -1)*(x - prev)
  y = project(y)
  prev = x; t=t_new
```
This is FISTA. Since objective nonconvex, may not guarantee but often accelerates. To be safe, we can run both: one function without momentum, one with momentum, under time slices; keep best. Or use momentum with a decay to 0 near end.

Let's implement exact projection with sort. Need handle target maybe 1.0 always; but for y in FISTA target1. For safety.

```
def project_simplex(v, target=1.0):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if v.size==0: return v
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
       return np.full(v.shape, target/v.size)
    if target <=0: return zeros
    # If sum within? Still project.
    # Fast path: if v all nonnegative and sum close target, normalize? Not exact but can save.
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s > 0 and abs(s-target) < 1e-12 and np.all(v >= -1e-15):
        out = np.maximum(v,0); out[out<0]=0; out /= out.sum() (if sum>0)
        return out
    # exact sort
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cv = np.cumsum(u)
    # rho: largest idx such that u[idx]*(idx+1) > cv[-1]-target
    ind = (u * (np.arange(1, u.size+1)) > (cv[-1] - target)).nonzero()[0]
    if ind.size==0:
       sigma = cv[-1]/u.size? If all below? For target positive impossible? fallback
    rho = ind[-1]
    sigma = (cv[rho] - target)/(rho+1.0)
    out = np.maximum(v - sigma, 0.0)
    s2=out.sum()
    if s2<=0: out=full(target/size)
    else: out *= target/s2
    return out
```
The sort projection exact only if target within sum positive. Normalize adjust.

For speed, we can implement a **two-point bisection** fallback? Sort okay.

In full_refine, many projections of `y - alpha*grad`. Since grad centered, y sum1, proposed sum maybe1 minus mean grad*alpha? mean grad ~0, so v already near sum1. Fast path likely triggers after projection? `np.all(v>=-1e-15)` may be false if many small negative. But still sort.

Maybe use approximate projection for line search to speed:
```
def project_approx(v,target=1.0):
   q=np.maximum(v,0.0)
   s=q.sum()
   if s>0: q*=target/s
   else: uniform
   return q
```
This is O(n) and fast. Use in line search, and exact project occasionally for accepted? The approximate may be sufficient. Let's use approx in line search to reduce cost. But if step large, q may be very different from exact; still maybe okay. We can set step small. For final best p, normalize_exact. I think approximate is safe for gradient descent on simplex near interior. It is actually the Euclidean projection onto the positive cone followed by scaling, not exact but common. It may step off exact normal but converges. Let's use it in line search and FISTA y update. It will be much faster (no sort). To be safe, at start of each full_refine, p=normalize_simplex. At end normalize. The score evaluates exact p (sum=1 after approximate). Approx projection preserves sum exactly. Good.

But if v has large negative, q zeros many entries; convex support might shrink. We can add small epsilon in normalization? `q = np.maximum(v, 0.0)`, if many zeros okay. If sum zero fallback.

Let's use `project_simplex_approx` throughout, and maybe exact `project_simplex` for block? Block doesn't project. For full, speed important.

Now, line search in full_refine approximate:
```
def project_simplex_approx(v,target=1.0):
   v=np.asarray(v,float)
   q=np.maximum(v,0.0)
   q[~finite]=0
   s=q.sum()
   if s<=0: return full
   return q*target/s
```
This is O(n).

Exact projection may be more accurate; we can use exact for y after momentum? no.

Let's consider effect of approximate on best score: Since renormalization after clipping is not exact projection, it can increase objective compared to exact. But line search uses trial values; okay. Let's implement `full_refine` with parameters `beta`, `max_iter`, `use_momentum`, `start_step`, `step_min`, `step_max`.
Pseudo:
```
def full_refine(p0, deadline, rng, beta=100000, max_iter=1000000, use_momentum=False, start_step=1.0, step_max=2.0, step_min=1e-8):
    p = normalize_simplex(p0.copy())
    n=p.size
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p)
    y=p.copy(); prev=None; t=1.0; step=start_step
    no_improve=0; it=0
    # if uniform, break symmetry
    if np.max(np.abs(p - 1/n))<1e-14: ...
    while it<max_iter and time.monotonic()<deadline:
       it+=1
       b=fft_convolution(y); G,w=smooth_max(b,beta)
       if not finite: break
       s=corr_pw(y,w); grad=2*s; grad -= np.mean(grad)
       gnorm=np.linalg.norm(grad)
       if gnorm <1e-14: break
       cur_obj = n*G  # use for line search? scale
       accepted=False
       trial=None; trial_obj=None; trial_b=None
       # Try alphas; maybe include a few random? We'll use multiples.
       for mult in (1,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001,0.0005,0.0002):
          alpha=step*mult
          x=project_approx(y - alpha*grad)
          xb=fft_convolution(x)
          Gx,w_x=smooth_max(xb,beta)
          objx=n*Gx
          if np.isfinite(objx) and objx < cur_obj - 1e-12:
             accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
          step=max(step_min, step*0.5)
          if step<step_min: break
          continue
       # accept
       if use_momentum:
          if prev is None: prev=x.copy()
          t_new=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t*t))
          y=x + (t/t_new -1)*(x-prev)
          y=project_approx(y)
          prev=x; t=t_new
       else:
          y=x
       scorex=2*n*np.max(xb)
       if scorex < best_score - 1e-12:
          best_score=scorex; best_p=x.copy(); no_improve=0
       else: no_improve+=1
       if no_improve>3000 and time? break maybe
       # adjust step
       if step < step_max: step=min(step_max, step*1.25)
       if it%1000==0: print(...)
    return best_p,best_score
```
But line search only tries decreasing from step. If step too small, it may stuck. We can reset step if no_improve large.
Also, if `objx < cur_obj` too strict with smooth and approximate projection, may reject if equal due numerical. Use `objx < cur_obj - 1e-14*max(1,cur_obj)`.
For high beta, G nearly exact max, differences small. Use tolerance `1e-13`.
Could be very slow if accepted rare. Maybe use a **fixed step with no line search** for some iterations to allow non-monotonic? But we need convergence. def shape_uniform(N): np.full(N,1)
def shape_linear(N, p=1): x=np.linspace(0,1,N); return (x**p + eps)
def shape_beta(N,a,b,eps=1e-6): x*np? return x**a*(1-x)**b + eps
def shape_exp(N, lam): x; exp(lam*x - max)
def shape_sine(N, A, freq, phase): 1 + A*np.sin(freq*2*np.pi*x + phase); clip positive + eps
def shape_cospi(N, B): 1 - B*np.cos(pi*x)
def shape_step(N,t,q): f=np.ones(N); f[:int(tN)] = 1+q; f[int(tN):] = 1-q; normalize (if q<1)
def shape_rand_smooth(N,rng, modes=10, scale=1): z = sum random cos/sin with freq 1..modes; p=exp(scale*tanh(z)+eps)

`smooth_max_and_weights` details:
```
def smooth_max_and_weights(b,beta):
   b=np.asarray(b,float); m=np.max(b)
   if m <=0 or beta <=0: return m, ones/m.size
   x=beta*(b-m); x=np.clip(x,-745,0); e=np.exp(x); Z=e.sum(); if Z<=0 ...; w=e/Z; G=m+log(Z)/beta; return G,w
```
When b has zeros and m small, okay.

`corr_pw(p,w)` verify indexing with small example to ensure derivative correct. Let's test small n=2, p=[a,b], bconv length3: [a^2,2ab,b^2]. For weight w=[w0,w1,w2], derivative wrt a: ∂b0/∂a=2a, ∂b1=2b, ∂b2=0 => 2(a w0 + b w1). wrt b: 2(b w2 + a w1). Our corr: need n=2,m=3, need=4? n+m-1=4, but actual conv length3. P length size8, WR? s = C[m-n : m][::-1] = C[1:3][::-1] length2. Does C index? This came from previous; likely yields [a w0+b w1, b w1?]. Let's trust but could be off by one? Let's derive: We want s_i = ∑_{j=0}^{m-1} p_j w_{i+j} for i=0..n-1. This is cross-correlation of p and w: s_i = ∑_j p_j w_{i+j}. If reverse w_r[t]=w[m-1-t]? Cross-correlation via convolution p * w_rev gives C[q]=∑ p_j w_rev[q-j]. Set q=m-1-i? Then w_rev[m-1-i-j]=w[i+j]. So s_i = C[m-1-i]. For i=0..n-1, q from m-1 down m-n. Since m=2n-1, m-n=n-1. So s = C[n-1:n+n-1?] reversed? The previous `C[m-n : m]` gives indices n-1 to 2n-2? length n. For n=2,m=3 => C[1:3] indices1,2; reversed [C2,C1]. But expected s0=C[2]? s1=C[1]? Yes s0 = C[m-1]=C2, s1=C[m-2]=C1. Good. For i=0, C2 =∑p_j w_rev[2-j]; w_rev[2-j]=w[m-1-(2-j)] = w[j]? For n=2,m=3: j=0 w0, j=1 w1. good. So previous correct.

`project_approx`:
```
def project_simplex_approx(v,target=1.0):
    v=np.asarray(v,float)
    q=np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    np.maximum(q,0.0,out=q)
    s=q.sum()
    if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): return full
    q *= target/s
    return q
```
This is fast. It may allocate out? Use `q = np.maximum(v,0)` simpler.

```
def block_search(best_p, deadline, rng, budget, B_list, alpha):
   n=best_p.size
   best_p, best_score = ...
   for B in B_list:
      if n % B !=0: continue
      if time > deadline: break
      # determine maxiter and starts
      if B <=64: starts = [p_to_block_c(best_p,B)] + [random_block_starts]
      else: starts = [best_c]
      beta_list = [20000,100000] maybe if B low; [100000,500000] high.
      for c0 in starts:
         c=c0.copy(); score=...
         for beta in beta_list:
            if time > deadline: break
            maxiter = 80 if B>512 else 200
            c=run_block_minimize(c,n,B,beta,bounds,maxiter)
            p=block_to_p; sc=fast_score(p)
            if sc<score update c
         update global best if improved
   return best
```

```
def make_initial_starts(N, seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    starts=[]
    x=np.linspace(0,1,N,dtype=np.float64)
    def add(p): ...
    add(np.ones(N))
    hs=... resize
    # powers
    for a in (0.3,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0):
       add((x+1e-6)**a + 1e-6)
       add((1-x+1e-6)**a + 1e-6)
    # beta (without symmetric too many)
    for a,b in [(0,1),(1,0),(0.3,1),(1,0.3),(0.3,2),(2,0.3),(0.7,1),(1,0.7)]:
       add(x**a*(1-x)**b+1e-8)
       add(x**b*(1-x)**a+1e-8)
    # exp
    for lam in (0.5,1,2,4,8):
       add(np.exp(lam*x-lam*x.max())+1e-8)
       add(np.exp(-lam*x)+1e-8)
    # cospi
    for B in (0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9):
       add(1 - B*np.cos(np.pi*x)+1e-8)
       add(1 + B*np.cos(np.pi*x)+1e-8)
    # sine2pi
    for A in (0.4,0.7,0.9):
       for phi in (0, np.pi/2, np.pi, 3*np.pi/2):
          add(1 + A*np.sin(2*np.pi*x+phi)+1e-8)
    # steps
    for t in (0.3,0.5,0.7):
       for q in (0.3,0.6,0.9):
          f=np.ones(N); f[:int(t*N)] = 1+q; f[int(t*N):]=1-q; add(f)
          f=np.ones(N); f[int(t*N):] = 1+q; f[:int(t*N)]=1-q; add(f)
    # random smooth 4
    for _ in range(4):
       z=np.zeros(N)
       for k in range(1,13):
          amp=rng.uniform(0.1,1.0)/k
          phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
          z += amp*np.cos(np.pi*k*x + phase)
       add(np.exp(0.7*z+1e-8))
    return starts
```

```
def full_refine(p0, deadline, rng, beta, max_iter, use_momentum, start_step):
    p=normalize_simplex(np.asarray(p0,float))
    n=p.size
    uniform = np.full(n,1.0/n)
    if np.max(np.abs(p-uniform))<1e-14:
       p=project_approx(p*(1+1e-4*np.sin(0.123*np.arange(n))))
    y=p.copy(); prev=None; t=1.0; step=start_step; best_p=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p)
    no_improve=0; it=0
    while it<max_iter and time.monotonic()<deadline:
       it+=1
       b=fft_convolution(y); G,w=smooth_max_and_weights(b,beta)
       if not np.isfinite(G): break
       cur_obj=n*G
       cur_score=2*n*float(np.max(b))
       s=corr_pw(y,w); grad=2*s; grad -= np.mean(grad)
       normg=float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
       if not np.isfinite(normg) or normg<1e-14:
           break
       accepted=False; x=None; scorex=best_score; objx=cur_obj
       for mult in (...):
          alpha=step*mult
          cand=y - alpha*grad
          x=project_simplex_approx(cand)
          xb=fft_convolution(x)
          Gx,_=smooth_max_and_weights(xb,beta)
          if not np.isfinite(Gx): continue
          objx=n*Gx
          scorex=2*n*float(np.max(xb))
          if objx < cur_obj - 1e-13 or scorex < cur_score - 1e-11:
              accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
          step = max(1e-8, 0.5*step)
          if step < 1e-8: break
          if no_improve > 10000:
             break
          continue
       # record if improves best
       if scorex < best_score - 1e-12:
          best_score=scorex; best_p=x.copy(); no_improve=0
       else:
          no_improve+=1
       if use_momentum:
          if prev is None:
             prev=x.copy()
             y=x
          else:
             t_new=0.5*(1.0+math.sqrt(1.0+4.0*t*t))
             mom=(t/t_new)-1.0
             y=x + mom*(x-prev)
             y=project_simplex_approx(y)
             prev=x; t=t_new
       else:
          y=x
       # adjust step
       if mult == 1.0: step = min(1.5, step*1.2)
       elif mult >=0.5: step = min(1.5, step*1.1)
       else: step = max(1e-8, step*0.8)
       if it % 5000 ==0: print(...)
       if no_improve > 3000:
          # restart from best, maybe perturb
          y=best_p.copy()
          if rng is not None:
             noise=0.01*rng.normal(size=n)
             y=project_simplex_approx(y*(1.0+noise))
          prev=None; t=1.0; step=start_step*0.5; no_improve=0
    return best_p,best_score
```

def block_search(initial_p, start_ps, deadline, rng, B_stages, budget_s):
   n=initial_p.size
   best_p=normalize_simplex(initial_p)
   best_score=fast_score(best_p)
   for B in B_stages:
      if B>n or n%B!=0: continue
      if time.monotonic() + 30 > deadline: break
      lim=12.0
      bounds=[(-lim,lim)]*max(0,B-1)
      # create c starts
      c_starts=[]
      c0=p_to_block_c(best_p,B); c_starts.append(c0)
      max_starts = 4 if B<=128 else 2
      for p0 in start_ps[:max_starts]:
         if time.monotonic()+20>deadline: break
         c0=p_to_block_c(p0,B); c_starts.append(c0)
      # maybe random
      if B<=256:
         c_starts.append(lim*0.3*rng.normal(0,1,B-1))
      beta_list = [20000,100000] if B<=256 else [100000,300000]
      if B>=2048: beta_list=[100000,500000]
      iter_count = block_maxiter(B,budget_s)
      for c0 in c_starts:
         if time.monotonic()+60 > deadline: break
         c=c0.copy()
         for beta in beta_list:
            if time.monotonic()+60 > deadline: break
            c=run_block_minimize(c,n,B,beta,bounds,iter_count)
            p=block_to_p(n,B,c)
            sc=fast_score(p)
            if finite and sc<best_score:
               best_score=sc; best_p=p.copy()
               print(...)
   return best_p,best_score
```

```
def sanitize_start(p):
   arr=np.asarray(p,float)
   if arr.size==0: return None
   arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
   arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
   s=arr.sum()
   if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): return None
   return arr/s
```

Implement:
```
def choose_top_seqs(seq_list,count):
   arrs=[]; scores=[]
   for seq in seq_list:
      p=sanitize_start(seq)
      if p is None: continue
      sc=fast_score(p)
      if np.isfinite(sc): p=normalize_simplex(p); arrs.append(p); scores.append(sc)
   if not arrs: return []
   idx=np.argsort(np.array(scores))[:count]
   return [arrs[i] for i in idx]

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing:

    2 * n * max_k (p * p)_k / (sum p)^2

for p >= 0, with p normalized to sum 1 during search.

The method uses:
  - FFT-based fast self-convolution and smooth max gradients.
  - A large pool of structured / random smooth initializations.
  - Coarse-to-fine L-BFGS optimization over piecewise-constant log-weights.
  - Full-resolution projected-gradient refinement with several beta values
    and optional Nesterov-style acceleration.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False


# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Basic fast primitives
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

def fft_convolution(a):
    """Linear convolution a*a, with tiny FFT negatives clipped to zero."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0] * a[0]], dtype=np.float64)

    need = 2 * n - 1
    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1

    F = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, size)[:need]
    b = np.ascontiguousarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    return np.maximum(b, 0.0)


def smooth_max_and_weights(b, beta):
    """Smooth maximum of b and softmax weights used for its gradient."""
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    if b.size == 0:
        return float("inf"), np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros_like(b, dtype=np.float64)

    if m <= 0.0 or beta <= 0.0:
        w = np.ones_like(b, dtype=np.float64)
        w /= float(b.size)
        return m, w

    x = beta * (b - m)
    x = np.clip(x, -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(x)
    Z = float(np.sum(e))

    if Z <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(Z):
        e = np.zeros_like(b, dtype=np.float64)
        e[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        Z = 1.0

    w = e / Z
    G = m + float(np.log(Z)) / beta
    return float(G), w


def corr_pw(p, w):
    """
    Return s_i = sum_j p_j w_{i+j}, for i=0..n-1.

    This is the unnormalized derivative information for the convolution
    max objective.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    m = w.size
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([float(np.dot(p, w[:1]))], dtype=np.float64)

    need = n + m - 1
    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1

    P = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    WR = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], size)
    C = np.fft.irfft(P * WR, size)[:need]

    # s_i = C[m-1-i].  With m = 2n-1 this is C[n-1 : 2n-1] reversed.
    s = C[m - n : m][::-1].copy()
    return np.ascontiguousarray(s, dtype=np.float64)


def fast_score(p):
    """Score used during search: 2*n*max(p*p), assuming sum(p)=1."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")

    S = float(np.sum(p))
    if S <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(S):
        return float("inf")

    b = fft_convolution(p)
    if b.size == 0:
        return float("inf")

    M = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(M):
        return float("inf")

    return float(2.0 * n * M) / (S * S)


def sanitize_start(p):
    """Convert a raw array into a nonnegative sum-1 sequence, or None."""
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    arr *= 1.0 / s
    return arr


def normalize_simplex(p):
    """Nonnegative normalization to sum 1, with fallback."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if p.size == 0:
        return p
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(p.shape, 1.0 / float(p.size), dtype=np.float64)
    p *= 1.0 / s
    return p


def project_simplex_approx(v, target=1.0):
    """
    Fast projection/scale onto {x>=0, sum(x)=target}.

    This is not the exact Euclidean projection, but it is much faster and is
    usually adequate for interior simplex refinement.
    """
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if v.size == 0:
        return v
    if target <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros_like(v, dtype=np.float64)

    q = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)

    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(q.shape, target / float(q.size), dtype=np.float64)

    q *= target / s
    return q


def resize_sequence(arr, n):
    """Resize a 1D sequence to length n by linear interpolation."""
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(max(1, n)), dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size == n:
        return arr
    if arr.size == 1:
        return np.full(n, arr[0], dtype=np.float64)

    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, arr.size, dtype=np.float64)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    return np.interp(xn, xo, arr).astype(np.float64)


def choose_top_seqs(seq_list, count):
    """Return up to count sequences with smallest fast_score."""
    arrs = []
    scores = []
    for seq in seq_list:
        p = sanitize_start(seq)
        if p is None:
            continue
        sc = fast_score(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            arrs.append(p)
            scores.append(float(sc))

    if not arrs:
        return []

    idx = np.argsort(np.asarray(scores, dtype=np.float64))[:max(0, int(count))]
    return [arrs[int(i)] for i in idx]


# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Initializations
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

def make_initial_starts(N, seed):
    """Generate a diverse set of nonnegative sum-1 starting profiles."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    starts = []
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, N, dtype=np.float64)

    def try_add(a):
        p = sanitize_start(a)
        if p is not None:
            starts.append(p)

    try_add(np.ones(N, dtype=np.float64))

    # Use previous state-of-the-art construction if present.
    hs = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if isinstance(hs, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)):
        arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64)
        if arr.size > 1:
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
            if float(np.sum(arr)) > 0.0:
                try_add(resize_sequence(arr, N))

    # Powers and beta-like shapes.
    for a in (0.25, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
        try_add((x + 1e-8) ** a)
        try_add((1.0 - x + 1e-8) ** a)

    for a, b in (
        (0.0, 1.0), (1.0, 0.0),
        (0.3, 1.0), (1.0, 0.3),
        (0.3, 2.0), (2.0, 0.3),
        (0.7, 1.0), (1.0, 0.7),
        (1.5, 0.4), (0.4, 1.5),
    ):
        try_add(x ** a * (1.0 - x) ** b + 1e-10)
        try_add(x ** b * (1.0 - x) ** a + 1e-10)

    # Exponentials.
    for lam in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0):
        try_add(np.exp(lam * (x - 1.0)) + 1e-10)
        try_add(np.exp(-lam * x) + 1e-10)

    # Low-frequency interval modes.
    for B in (0.25, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 0.95):
        try_add(1.0 - B * np.cos(np.pi * x) + 1e-10)
        try_add(1.0 + B * np.cos(np.pi * x) + 1e-10)

    # Periodic first harmonic modes.
    for A in (0.35, 0.6, 0.85):
        for phi in (0.0, 0.5 * np.pi, np.pi, 1.5 * np.pi):
            try_add(1.0 + A * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x + phi) + 1e-10)

    # Two-level asymmetric steps.
    for t in (0.25, 0.35, 0.5, 0.65, 0.75):
        for q in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 0.9):
            f = np.ones(N, dtype=np.float64)
            m = int(t * N)
            f[:m] = 1.0 + q
            f[m:] = max(1e-6, 1.0 - q)
            try_add(f)

            f = np.ones(N, dtype=np.float64)
            f[:m] = max(1e-6, 1.0 - q)
            f[m:] = 1.0 + q
            try_add(f)

    # Piecewise-linear valley/peak shapes.
    try_add(1.0 - 0.8 * np.abs(x - 0.5))
    try_add(1.0 + 0.8 * np.abs(x - 0.5))
    try_add(1.0 - 0.9 * np.sin(np.pi * x))
    try_add(1.0 + 0.9 * np.sin(np.pi * x))

    # Smooth random Fourier-like profiles.
    for _ in range(8):
        z = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
        for k in range(1, 13):
            amp = rng.uniform(0.10, 1.00) / float(k)
            phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            z += amp * np.cos(np.pi * k * x + phase)
        try_add(np.exp(0.65 * np.tanh(z)) + 1e-10)

    # Random nonnegative perturbations of uniform.
    for _ in range(4):
        z = rng.normal(0.0, 0.8, N)
        try_add(np.exp(z) + 1e-10)

    return starts


# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Piecewise-constant block optimization
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

def block_p_and_wb(c, n, B):
    """Convert block log weights c to full sequence p and block weights wb."""
    L = max(1, n // B)

    z = np.empty(B, dtype=np.float64)
    if B > 1:
        c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        tmp = np.zeros(B - 1, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(tmp.size, c.size)
        if m:
            tmp[:m] = c[:m]
        z[:-1] = tmp
    z[-1] = 0.0

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        z = np.zeros(B, dtype=np.float64)

    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    wb = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(wb))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        wb = np.ones(B, dtype=np.float64) / float(B)
    else:
        wb *= 1.0 / s

    H = wb / float(L)
    p = np.repeat(H, L)
    if p.size != n:
        # Safety resize if divisibility assumption is broken.
        p = np.resize(p, n)
    return p, wb


def block_to_p(n, B, c):
    p, _ = block_p_and_wb(c, n, B)
    return p


def p_to_block_c(p, n, B):
    """Convert a full sequence to block log weights for the same B."""
    L = max(1, n // B)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if p.size != n:
        p = resize_sequence(p, n)

    if B <= 1:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    block = p.reshape(B, L).mean(axis=1)
    block = np.maximum(block, 1e-300)
    logb = np.log(block)
    c = logb[:-1] - logb[-1]
    return np.clip(c, -12.0, 12.0).astype(np.float64)


def block_objective(c, n, B, beta):
    """
    Objective n * smooth_max(p*p) and gradient with respect to c.

    The n factor is kept so that the objective is O(1).
    """
    if B <= 1:
        p = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
        b = fft_convolution(p)
        G, _ = smooth_max_and_weights(b, beta)
        if not np.isfinite(G):
            return float("inf"), np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
        return float(n * G), np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    p, wb = block_p_and_wb(c, n, B)

    b = fft_convolution(p)
    G, w = smooth_max_and_weights(b, beta)
    if not np.isfinite(G):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros(B - 1, dtype=np.float64)

    s = corr_pw(p, w)
    g = 2.0 * np.maximum(s, 0.0)

    L = max(1, n // B)
    Gsum = g.reshape(B, L).sum(axis=1)
    Wavg = float(np.dot(wb, Gsum))
    grad = wb * (Gsum - Wavg) / float(L)
    grad = n * grad

    grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    grad = np.clip(grad, -1e6, 1e6)

    val = float(n * G)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        val = float("inf")

    return val, grad[:-1].astype(np.float64)


def run_block_minimize(c0, n, B, beta, bounds, maxiter):
    """Robust L-BFGS-B minimization in the block representation."""
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        c = np.asarray(c0, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        c = np.clip(c, -12.0, 12.0)
        return c

    c0 = np.asarray(c0, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    dim = max(0, B - 1)
    if c0.size != dim:
        tmp = np.zeros(dim, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(tmp.size, c0.size)
        if m:
            tmp[:m] = c0[:m]
        c0 = tmp

    c0 = np.clip(c0, -12.0, 12.0)

    if dim == 0:
        return c0

    options = {
        "maxiter": int(maxiter),
        "ftol": 1e-12,
        "gtol": (1e-5 if B < 512 else 3e-4),
        "maxls": 30,
        "maxcor": 20,
    }

    try:
        res = minimize(
            block_objective,
            c0,
            args=(n, B, beta),
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            jac=True,
            bounds=bounds,
            options=options,
        )
        x = getattr(res, "x", None)
        c = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64) if x is not None else c0
    except Exception:
        c = c0

    if c.size != dim:
        tmp = np.zeros(dim, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(tmp.size, c.size)
        if m:
            tmp[:m] = c[:m]
        c = tmp

    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    return np.clip(c, -12.0, 12.0).astype(np.float64)


def block_maxiter(B, budget_s):
    """Rough per-stage L-BFGS iteration budget."""
    if B <= 16:
        m = 260
    elif B <= 64:
        m = 180
    elif B <= 256:
        m = 130
    elif B <= 1024:
        m = 90
    else:
        m = 65

    if budget_s > 2000.0:
        m = int(m * 1.7)
    elif budget_s > 1000.0:
        m = int(m * 1.3)

    return max(30, m)


def block_search(initial_p, start_ps, deadline, rng, B_stages, budget_s):
    """Coarse-to-fine piecewise-constant refinement."""
    n = np.asarray(initial_p, dtype=np.float64).size
    best_p = normalize_simplex(initial_p)
    best_score = fast_score(best_p)

    lim = 12.0

    for B in B_stages:
        if B > n or (n % B) != 0:
            continue

        if B <= 1:
            continue

        if time.monotonic() + 45.0 > deadline:
            break

        bounds = [(-lim, lim)] * (B - 1)
        maxiter = block_maxiter(B, budget_s)

        c_starts = []
        c0 = p_to_block_c(best_p, n, B)
        c_starts.append(c0)

        max_extra_starts = 4 if B <= 128 else (2 if B <= 1024 else 1)
        for p0 in start_ps[:max_extra_starts]:
            if time.monotonic() + 25.0 > deadline:
                break
            c0 = p_to_block_c(p0, n, B)
            c_starts.append(c0)

        if B <= 256:
            c_starts.append(0.8 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, max(0, B - 1)))

        if B <= 256:
            beta_list = (20000.0, 80000.0, 250000.0)
        elif B <= 1024:
            beta_list = (50000.0, 150000.0, 400000.0)
        else:
            beta_list = (100000.0, 300000.0, 800000.0)

        for c_start in c_starts:
            if time.monotonic() + 70.0 > deadline:
                break

            local_c = np.asarray(c_start, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            local_p = block_to_p(n, B, local_c)
            local_sc = fast_score(local_p)
            if not np.isfinite(local_sc):
                continue

            for beta in beta_list:
                if time.monotonic() + 90.0 > deadline:
                    break

                c_new = run_block_minimize(local_c, n, B, beta, bounds, maxiter)
                p_new = block_to_p(n, B, c_new)
                sc_new = fast_score(p_new)

                if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < local_sc:
                    local_sc = sc_new
                    local_c = c_new
                    local_p = p_new

            if np.isfinite(local_sc) and local_sc < best_score:
                best_score = float(local_sc)
                best_p = local_p.copy()
                print(f"block B={B:5d} score={best_score:.8f}")

    return best_p, best_score


# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Full-resolution refinement
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

def full_refine(p0, deadline, rng, beta, use_momentum=False, start_step=1.0):
    """Projected smooth-max descent on the full simplex."""
    p = normalize_simplex(np.asarray(p0, dtype=np.float64).copy())
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p, float("inf")

    # Break perfect symmetry if needed.
    uniform = 1.0 / float(n)
    if n > 1 and float(np.max(np.abs(p - uniform))) < 1e-14:
        t = 0.123 * np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
        p = project_simplex_approx(p * (1.0 + 1e-4 * np.sin(t)))

    y = p.copy()
    prev = None
    t_mom = 1.0
    step = float(start_step)
    min_step = 1e-8
    max_step = 1.6

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(p)
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    mults = (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02, 0.01, 0.005, 0.002, 0.001)

    while it < 5000000 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        b = fft_convolution(y)
        G, w = smooth_max_and_weights(b, beta)
        if not np.isfinite(G):
            break

        cur_obj = float(n) * float(G)
        cur_score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(b))

        s = corr_pw(y, w)
        grad = 2.0 * np.maximum(s, 0.0)
        grad -= float(np.mean(grad))

        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if not np.isfinite(gnorm) or gnorm < 1e-14:
            break

        accepted = False
        x = None
        scorex = float("inf")
        used_mult = 1.0

        for mult in mults:
            alpha = step * mult
            cand = y - alpha * grad
            x = project_simplex_approx(cand)
            xb = fft_convolution(x)
            Gx, _ = smooth_max_and_weights(xb, beta)
            if not np.isfinite(Gx):
                continue

            objx = float(n) * float(Gx)
            scorex = float(2.0 * n * np.max(xb))

            if objx < cur_obj - 1e-13 or scorex < cur_score - 1e-11:
                accepted = True
                used_mult = mult
                break

        if not accepted:
            step = max(min_step, 0.5 * step)
            if step <= min_step:
                break
            if no_improve > 12000:
                break
            continue

        if np.isfinite(scorex) and scorex < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = float(scorex)
            best_p = x.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if use_momentum:
            if prev is None:
                prev = x.copy()
                y = x
            else:
                t_new = 0.5 * (1.0 + math.sqrt(1.0 + 4.0 * t_mom * t_mom))
                mom = (t_mom / t_new) - 1.0
                y = x + mom * (x - prev)
                y = project_simplex_approx(y)
                prev = x
                t_mom = t_new
        else:
            y = x

        if used_mult >= 1.0:
            step = min(max_step, step * 1.25)
        elif used_mult >= 0.5:
            step = min(max_step, step * 1.10)
        else:
            step = max(min_step, step * 0.78)

        if it % 5000 == 0:
            print(
                f"full beta={beta:.3e} it={it} best={best_score:.8f} step={step:.3e} mom={use_momentum}"
            )

        if no_improve > 2500:
            y = best_p.copy()
            if rng is not None:
                eps = 0.004 if use_momentum else 0.008
                z = rng.normal(0.0, eps, n)
                y = project_simplex_approx(y * (1.0 + z))
            prev = None
            t_mom = 1.0
            step = max(min_step, 0.5 * start_step)
            no_improve = 0

    return best_p, best_score


# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Main search entry point
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """
    Return a list of non-negative floats minimizing the given objective.

    This is the function invoked by the grading harness.
    """
    try:
        seed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        seed = 42

    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0

    if budget_s <= 0.0:
        return [1.0]

    start_time = time.monotonic()

    if budget_s < 120.0:
        N = 2048
        reserve = 20.0
    elif budget_s < 600.0:
        N = 4096
        reserve = 45.0
    else:
        N = 8192
        reserve = 140.0

    deadline = start_time + max(60.0, budget_s - reserve)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    print(f"Search budget={budget_s:.1f}s, N={N}")

    # ---- Initial candidates -------------------------------------------------
    raw_starts = make_initial_starts(N, seed + 1)
    start_ps = choose_top_seqs(raw_starts, 10)

    if not start_ps:
        return [float(1.0) for _ in range(N)]

    best_p = start_ps[0].copy()
    best_score = fast_score(best_p)
    print(f"initial best score={best_score:.8f}")

    # ---- Coarse-to-fine block optimization ----------------------------------
    B_base = [4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512, 1024]
    if budget_s > 1500.0:
        B_base.append(2048)
    if budget_s > 2100.0:
        B_base.append(4096)

    B_stages = [B for B in B_base if B <= N and (N % B) == 0]

    block_deadline = min(deadline, start_time + max(60.0, 0.34 * budget_s))
    if block_deadline - time.monotonic() > 80.0:
        best_p, best_score = block_search(
            best_p,
            start_ps[:8],
            block_deadline,
            rng,
            B_stages,
            budget_s,
        )
        print(f"after block search score={best_score:.8f}")

    # ---- Full refinement schedule -------------------------------------------
    rem0 = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())

    # Fractions of the remaining time assigned to each full refinement stage.
    stages = (
        # beta, use_momentum, fraction
        (20000.0, False, 0.12),
        (80000.0, True, 0.22),
        (200000.0, False, 0.20),
        (500000.0, True, 0.20),
        (1000000.0, False, 0.14),
        (1500000.0, False, 0.12),
    )

    for beta, use_momentum, frac in stages:
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining <= 80.0:
            break

        # Do not let a single stage consume more than 700 seconds.
        alloc = min(700.0, max(25.0, remaining * frac))
        sub_deadline = time.monotonic() + alloc
        if sub_deadline > deadline:
            sub_deadline = deadline - 1.0

        p_new, sc_new = full_refine(
            best_p,
            sub_deadline,
            rng,
            beta,
            use_momentum=use_momentum,
            start_step=1.0 if beta < 300000.0 else 0.5,
        )

        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc_new)
            best_p = p_new.copy()
            print(f"full refine beta={beta:.3e} mom={use_momentum} score={best_score:.8f}")
        else:
            print(f"full refine beta={beta:.3e} mom={use_momentum} no improvement")

    # ---- A few short random restarts from the best basin --------------------
    for _ in range(3):
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining <= 80.0:
            break

        alloc = min(180.0, max(25.0, remaining * 0.10))
        sub_deadline = time.monotonic() + alloc
        if sub_deadline > deadline:
            sub_deadline = deadline - 1.0

        perturb_scale = 0.01
        z = rng.normal(0.0, perturb_scale, N)
        p_start = project_simplex_approx(best_p * (1.0 + z))

        p_new, sc_new = full_refine(
            p_start,
            sub_deadline,
            rng,
            beta=1000000.0,
            use_momentum=False,
            start_step=0.4,
        )

        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc_new)
            best_p = p_new.copy()
            print(f"restart polish score={best_score:.8f}")

    # ---- Final cleanup -------------------------------------------------------
    final_p = normalize_simplex(best_p)
    final_p = np.maximum(final_p, 0.0)
    final_p = np.minimum(final_p, 1000.0)

    s = float(np.sum(final_p))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        final_p = np.full(N, 1.0 / float(N), dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(np.sum(final_p))

    if s > 0.0:
        final_p *= 1.0 / s

    final_seq = [float(x) for x in final_p]

    # Official check / fallback.
    try:
        official = float(evaluate_sequence(final_seq))
    except Exception:
        official = float("inf")

    if np.isfinite(official) and official < best_score:
        best_score = official
        print(f"official score={best_score:.8f}")

    if (not np.isfinite(official)) or official > 2.0 + 1e-9:
        # Uniform sequence gives score exactly 2 and is always safe.
        return [1.0 / float(N)] * N

    return final_seq
```
